{"type": "user", "message": {"role": "user", "content": [{"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": "toolu_reference_0001", "is_error": false, "content": [{"type": "text", "text": "MBC/ALD COA 2026 — laytime, demurrage and despatch (clauses 14 to 24)\nContract of affreightment for steam coal, Baltic load ports, calendar 2026, between Aldwick Shipping AS, Bergen (Owners) and Merrow Bulk Carriers Ltd, Leith (Charterers). Signed 14 January 2026. Fixture recaps for each nomination incorporate these clauses unchanged.\nOwners: Aldwick Shipping AS. Charterers: Merrow Bulk Carriers Ltd. Cargo: steam coal. Currency of account: USD.\nClause 14 — Notice of readiness\nNotice of readiness may be tendered at any time, day or night, by e-mail to the shippers or receivers and to charterers' agents, once the vessel has arrived at the port and is in all respects ready, whether in berth or not (WIBON), whether in port or not, whether customs cleared or not.\nClause 15 — Commencement of laytime\nLaytime shall commence 12 running hours after notice of readiness has been tendered (turn time), unless loading sooner commences, in which case time shall count from the commencement of loading. Turn time runs whatever the day of the week.\nClause 16 — Rate\nThe cargo shall be loaded at the average rate of 12,000 metric tonnes per weather working day of 24 consecutive hours, Saturdays, Sundays and holidays included (SHINC). The rate applies to the bill of lading quantity.\nClause 17 — Weather working day\n'Weather working day' means a day of 24 consecutive hours on which time counts unless, and only for so long as, weather actually prevents the loading of the vessel.\nClause 18 — Shifting\nTime used in shifting from the anchorage or waiting place to the first berth, and between berths, shall not count as laytime, from anchor aweigh or letting go until all fast at the next berth. Shifting expenses for Owners' account from anchorage to first berth; between berths for Charterers' account.\nClause 19 — Completion\nLaytime shall end on completion of loading. Time for the issue of documents, any draft survey after completion, unmooring and sailing formalities shall not count.\nClause 20 — Demurrage\nCharterers shall pay demurrage at the rate of USD 15,000 per day or pro rata for any part of a day for all time by which the laytime allowed is exceeded.\nClause 21 — Despatch\nOwners shall pay despatch money at half the demurrage rate, USD 7,500 per day pro rata, on laytime saved.\nClause 22 — Stevedores and shore installations\nStevedores at the loading port shall be appointed and paid by Charterers or their shippers/receivers and shall work under the Master's supervision. The shore installations (conveyors, shiploaders, grabs, stockpiles and hoppers) are provided by the terminal at Charterers' arrangement.\nClause 23 — Statement of facts and claims\nThe agents' statement of facts, signed by the Master and the agents, shall be the record of the times of the call. Claims for demurrage shall be presented within 90 days of completion of discharge with the statement of facts and a laytime statement, failing which the claim shall be deemed waived.\nClause 24 — Law and arbitration\nEnglish law; London arbitration, LMAA Terms current at the date of the reference; small claims procedure below USD 100,000 or equivalent.\nSignature block\nFor Owners: Aldwick Shipping AS (signed). For Charterers: Merrow Bulk Carriers Ltd, Hedda Lisle, Chief Financial Officer (signed)."}]}]}}

{"type": "user", "message": {"role": "user", "content": [{"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": "toolu_reference_0002", "is_error": false, "content": [{"type": "text", "text": "# MBC/HOL Grainvoy 2026 — laytime, demurrage and despatch (clauses 14 to 24)\n\nVoyage charter form for milling wheat from French, Belgian and Black Sea ports, 2026 programme, between Holmfirth Bulk Ltd, Hull (Owners) and Merrow Bulk Carriers Ltd (Charterers). Signed 2 March 2026.\n\nOwners: Holmfirth Bulk Ltd. Charterers: Merrow Bulk Carriers Ltd. Cargo: milling wheat. Currency of account: USD.\n\n### Clause 14 — Notice of readiness\n\nNotice of readiness may be tendered at any time, day or night, by e-mail to the shippers or receivers and to charterers' agents, once the vessel has arrived at the port and is in all respects ready, whether in berth or not (WIBON), whether in port or not, whether customs cleared or not.\n\n### Clause 15 — Commencement of laytime\n\nLaytime shall commence 6 running hours after notice of readiness has been tendered (turn time), unless loading sooner commences, in which case time shall count from the commencement of loading. Turn time runs whatever the day of the week.\n\n### Clause 16 — Rate\n\nThe cargo shall be loaded at the average rate of 8,000 metric tonnes per weather working day of 24 consecutive hours, Sundays and holidays excepted, even if used (SHEX EIU): the period from 12:00 on Saturday to 08:00 on Monday, and any local public holiday at the port from 00:00 to 24:00, shall not count as laytime whether or not the vessel works. The rate applies to the bill of lading quantity.\n\n### Clause 17 — Weather working day\n\n'Weather working day' means a day of 24 consecutive hours on which time counts unless, and only for so long as, weather actually prevents the loading of the vessel.\n\n### Clause 18 — Shifting\n\nTime used in shifting from the anchorage or waiting place to the first berth, and between berths, shall not count as laytime, from anchor aweigh or letting go until all fast at the next berth. Shifting expenses for Owners' account from anchorage to first berth; between berths for Charterers' account.\n\n### Clause 19 — Completion\n\nLaytime shall end on completion of loading. Time for the issue of documents, any draft survey after completion, unmooring and sailing formalities shall not count.\n\n### Clause 20 — Demurrage\n\nCharterers shall pay demurrage at the rate of USD 18,500 per day or pro rata for any part of a day for all time by which the laytime allowed is exceeded.\n\n### Clause 21 — Despatch\n\nOwners shall pay despatch money at half the demurrage rate, USD 9,250 per day pro rata, on laytime saved.\n\n### Clause 22 — Stevedores and shore installations\n\nStevedores at the loading port shall be appointed and paid by Charterers or their shippers/receivers and shall work under the Master's supervision. The shore installations (conveyors, shiploaders, grabs, stockpiles and hoppers) are provided by the terminal at Charterers' arrangement.\n\n### Clause 23 — Statement of facts and claims\n\nThe agents' statement of facts, signed by the Master and the agents, shall be the record of the times of the call. Claims for demurrage shall be presented within 90 days of completion of discharge with the statement of facts and a laytime statement, failing which the claim shall be deemed waived.\n\n### Clause 24 — Law and arbitration\n\nEnglish law; London arbitration, LMAA Terms current at the date of the reference; small claims procedure below USD 100,000 or equivalent.\n\n### Signature block\n\nFor Owners: Holmfirth Bulk Ltd (signed). For Charterers: Merrow Bulk Carriers Ltd, Hedda Lisle, Chief Financial Officer (signed).\n"}]}]}}

Signed 19 February 2026. All sums under this charter are in euro.\nOwners: Kestrel Maritime BV. Charterers: Merrow Bulk Carriers Ltd. Cargo: granular urea. Currency of account: EUR.\nClause 14 — Notice of readiness\nNotice of readiness may be tendered at any time, day or night, by e-mail to the shippers or receivers and to charterers' agents, once the vessel has arrived at the port and is in all respects ready, whether in berth or not (WIBON), whether in port or not, whether customs cleared or not.\nClause 15 — Commencement of laytime\nLaytime shall commence 12 running hours after notice of readiness has been tendered (turn time), unless discharging sooner commences, in which case time shall count from the commencement of discharging. Turn time runs whatever the day of the week.\nClause 16 — Rate\nThe cargo shall be discharged at the average rate of 5,000 metric tonnes per weather working day of 24 consecutive hours, Sundays excepted, even if used: the period from 17:00 on Saturday to 08:00 on Monday shall not count as laytime whether or not the vessel works. Public holidays are not excepted under this charter and count as ordinary days. The rate applies to the bill of lading quantity.\nClause 17 — Weather working day\n'Weather working day' means a day of 24 consecutive hours on which time counts unless, and only for so long as, weather actually prevents the discharging of the vessel.\nClause 18 — Shifting\nTime used in shifting from the anchorage or waiting place to the first berth, and between berths, shall not count as laytime, from anchor aweigh or letting go until all fast at the next berth. Shifting expenses for Owners' account from anchorage to first berth; between berths for Charterers' account.\nClause 19 — Completion\nLaytime shall end on completion of discharging. Time for the issue of documents, any draft survey after completion, unmooring and sailing formalities shall not count.\nClause 20 — Demurrage\nCharterers shall pay demurrage at the rate of EUR 12,000 per day or pro rata for any part of a day for all time by which the laytime allowed is exceeded.\nClause 21 — Despatch\nOwners shall pay despatch money at half the demurrage rate, EUR 6,000 per day pro rata, on laytime saved.\nClause 22 — Stevedores and shore installations\nStevedores at the discharging port shall be appointed and paid by Charterers or their shippers/receivers and shall work under the Master's supervision. The shore installations (conveyors, shiploaders, grabs, stockpiles and hoppers) are provided by the terminal at Charterers' arrangement.\nClause 23 — Statement of facts and claims\nThe agents' statement of facts, signed by the Master and the agents, shall be the record of the times of the call. Claims for demurrage shall be presented within 90 days of completion of discharge with the statement of facts and a laytime statement, failing which the claim shall be deemed waived.\nClause 24 — Law and arbitration\nEnglish law; London arbitration, LMAA Terms current at the date of the reference; small claims procedure below USD 100,000 or equivalent.\nSignature block\nFor Owners: Kestrel Maritime BV (signed). For Charterers: Merrow Bulk Carriers Ltd, Hedda Lisle, Chief Financial Officer (signed)."}]}]}}

{"type": "user", "message": {"role": "user", "content": [{"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": "toolu_reference_0005", "is_error": false, "content": [{"type": "text", "text": "# Port notes — Antwerp, Belgium (claims desk reference, rev. June 2026)\n\nTerminal: Kaai 526 fertiliser terminal; night gangs supplied by the pool on 24 hours' notice.\n\nPublic holidays relevant to Q3 2026: 15 August — outside the September call.\n\nWorking pattern: terminals work 24 hours, seven days, unless the berth schedule says otherwise; weekend work does not change how a charter's excepted periods are treated.\nPilotage and towage: compulsory; the time from anchor aweigh to all fast is logged by the agents.\nAgents: charterers' nominated agents log statements of facts from the vessel's arrival to sailing and file them in Box within 10 days.\n\n## Merrow calls here in 2026\n- PC-3107 MV Kestrel Bay (MBC/KES Fertvoy 2026), 21,000 MT granular urea, berth Kaai 526.\n\n## Port authority berth list (extract, generic numbering)\n\n| Berth | Max draft (m) | Max LOA (m) | Equipment | Published rate (t/day) |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Antwerp berth 1 | 12.5 | 225 | travelling shiploader | 18,000 |\n| Antwerp berth 2 | 13.3 | 240 | two mobile harbour cranes | 9,000 |\n| Antwerp berth 3 | 14.1 | 255 | grab unloader / spout | 12,000 |\n\n## Earlier 2026 calls (settled)\n- PC-2105 MV Kestrel Bay, 20,000 MT, demurrage EUR 4500.00 (disputed).\n\n## Local practice notes\n- Draft surveys: initial and final surveys by an independent surveyor; an intermediate survey may be requested by shippers.\n- Meal breaks: gangs stop for 30-60 minutes around midday and midnight in most ports; time counts.\n- Rain: open-hatch cargoes (grain, urea, coal fines) are protected by closing hatches; ore pellets are normally worked in light rain.\n- Equipment: shiploader and conveyor faults are logged by the terminal's control room and reported to the agents.\n- Night work: gangs for night shifts are ordered by the agents on the day; the pool confirms by 16:00.\n- Documents: bills of lading are signed by the agents on the master's authority once the final figure is agreed.\n- Waiting: when the berth is occupied the vessel waits at the anchorage named in the agents' line-up; NOR is tendered on arrival there.\n"}]}]}}

June 2026)\n\nTerminal: LKAB Fagernes ore piers 4-6; shiploaders SL-1 (pier 5) and SL-2 (pier 6).\n\nPublic holidays relevant to Q3 2026: None in August.\n\nWorking pattern: terminals work 24 hours, seven days, unless the berth schedule says otherwise; weekend work does not change how a charter's excepted periods are treated.\nPilotage and towage: compulsory; the time from anchor aweigh to all fast is logged by the agents.\nAgents: charterers' nominated agents log statements of facts from the vessel's arrival to sailing and file them in Box within 10 days.\n\n## Merrow calls here in 2026\n- PC-3110 MV Tarnby Ore (MBC/TAR Orevoy 2026), 90,000 MT iron ore pellets, berth LKAB Fagernes ore pier 5.\n\n## Port authority berth list (extract, generic numbering)\n\n| Berth | Max draft (m) | Max LOA (m) | Equipment | Published rate (t/day) |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Narvik berth 1 | 12.5 | 225 | travelling shiploader | 18,000 |\n| Narvik berth 2 | 13.3 | 240 | two mobile harbour cranes | 9,000 |\n| Narvik berth 3 | 14.1 | 255 | grab unloader / spout | 12,000 |\n\n## Earlier 2026 calls (settled)\n- PC-2107 MV Tarnby Ore, 90,000 MT, demurrage USD 18000.00 (agreed).\n\n## Local practice notes\n- Draft surveys: initial and final surveys by an independent surveyor; an intermediate survey may be requested by shippers.\n- Meal breaks: gangs stop for 30-60 minutes around midday and midnight in most ports; time counts.\n- Rain: open-hatch cargoes (grain, urea, coal fines) are protected by closing hatches; ore pellets are normally worked in light rain.\n- Equipment: shiploader and conveyor faults are logged by the terminal's control room and reported to the agents.\n- Night work: gangs for night shifts are ordered by the agents on the day; the pool confirms by 16:00.\n- Documents: bills of lading are signed by the agents on the master's authority once the final figure is agreed.\n- Waiting: when the berth is occupied the vessel waits at the anchorage named in the agents' line-up; NOR is tendered on arrival there.\n"}]}]}}

{"type": "user", "message": {"role": "user", "content": [{"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": "toolu_reference_0015", "is_error": false, "content": [{"type": "text", "text": "# Stevedoring and terminal arrangements under the 2026 charters (claims desk reference)\n\nAll four 2026 charters are on terms where cargo is put on board / taken off by Charterers' arrangement:\n\n1. Stevedores are appointed and paid by Merrow or by its shippers/receivers (clause 22 of each charter). A gang that is not\n   supplied, arrives late or is short is a matter on Charterers' side of the contract.\n2. Shore installations — conveyors, shiploaders, grabs, hoppers, stockpiles and the supply of cargo to the berth — are the\n   terminal's, provided at Charterers' arrangement. A breakdown or a cargo shortfall at the terminal is on Charterers' side.\n3. None of the four charters contains a breakdown, strike or 'cargo not ready' exception. Such stoppages are not among the\n   charter exceptions and laytime continues to run through them.\n4. Ship's gear: all four Q3 vessels on the ore and coal contracts are gearless; the Kestrel and Holmfirth vessels used shore\n   cranes and spouts only. No ship's-gear breakdowns are recorded in Q3.\n5. Costs of shore stoppages (terminal standby, extra shifts) are recovered from shippers/receivers under the sale contracts\n   by the commercial team; they never appear on the laytime statement.\n\nTerminal incident reports go to operations (Sefa Ndlovu), not to the claims desk.\n\nPer contract:\n- Aldwick COA (coal, Baltic): shippers' terminals at Ventspils, Gdansk and Riga load by conveyor and travelling\n  shiploader; the stevedoring is part of the terminal service.\n- Holmfirth grain programme: silo terminals at Rouen, Constanta and Ghent; cargo arrives by truck and rail and is held\n  in the terminal's stockpile/silo before loading; spouts are terminal equipment.\n- Kestrel fertiliser discharges: receivers' stevedores from the port labour pool; mobile harbour cranes and hoppers are\n  the terminal's; night gangs are ordered by the agents on receivers' behalf.\n- Tarnby ore programme: LKAB / Malmhamnen ore terminals with fixed shiploaders; ore is conveyed from the stockyard.\n"}]}]}}

Applies to every call where Merrow is charterer under the 2026 contracts.\\n\\n1. Sources. The charter party clauses (14-24) govern. The agents' statement of facts for the call, filed in Box (Merrow Claims / Statements of Facts / <quarter>), is the record of times. The B/L quantity is the one on the claims register (Port Calls). The owners' laytime statement is their position and is never a source for our figure.\\n\\n2. Laytime allowed. B/L quantity divided by the charter's daily rate, in days of 24 hours; work in minutes.\\n\\n3. Start. Turn time runs from the tender of NOR; laytime starts when it expires, unless cargo work started sooner, in which case from commencement (clause 15). If that moment falls in an excepted period, laytime starts when the excepted period ends.\\n\\n4. Excepted periods. Only the charter's exceptions interrupt laytime: the weekend and holiday hours where the charter excepts them (clause 16), shifting from anchor aweigh / letting go to all fast (clause 18), and weather under clause 17. Overlapping exceptions count once.\\n\\n5. Weather. Clause 17 of each charter governs: time counts unless, and only for so long as, weather actually prevents the cargo being worked. The test is one of cause: a minute is weather time only if, the weather apart, the cargo would have been worked in it. In the statements of facts it most often shows as the hatches being closed for rain and reopened when it clears. Light rain, showers or drizzle with work continuing are not deductions.\\n\\n6. Other stoppages. Stoppages for any other cause — a breakdown of terminal equipment, cargo not at the berth, stevedores, surveys, meal breaks, documents — are on Charterers' side under our charters and do not interrupt laytime (see the stevedoring and terminal note).\\n\\n7. End. Laytime ends at completion of loading or discharging (clause 19). Documents, final draft survey and sailing do not count.\\n\\n8. Once on demurrage. When the laytime allowed has been used up, all further time to completion is demurrage. None of the laytime exceptions (weather, weekends, holidays, shifting) applies to time on demurrage under our charters.\\n\\n9. Money. Demurrage = rate per day x minutes on demurrage / 1440. Despatch = half the demurrage rate x minutes of laytime saved / 1440. Round to cents (half up) at the end only. Currency as the charter says; never convert.\\n\\n10. Time used. Time used = laytime counted + time on demurrage. Time saved/lost = minutes on demurrage, or minutes of laytime saved. Record hours to two decimals.\\n\\n11. Agreement. Compare our amount with the owners' claimed amount (their laytime statement in the claims mailbox). Equal to the cent: Agreed. Any difference: Disputed, with a dispute note in Jira LTD.\\n\\n12. Communications. The desk never sends positions, statements or notes to owners, brokers or agents, and never touches invoices in Xero (see the communications policy).\", \"representation\": \"storage\"}}}, {\"id\": \"941003\", \"type\": \"page\", \"status\": \"current\", \"title\": \"Laytime claims summary 2026-Q1\", \"space\": {\"key\": \"CLM\"}, \"version\": {\"number\": 2}, \"history\": {\"createdBy\": {\"username\": \"Tobias Wrenfield\"}, \"createdDate\": \"2026-05-19T09:00:00Z\"}, \"_links\": {\"webui\": \"/spaces/CLM/pages/941003\"}, \"ancestors\": [{\"id\": \"941001\", \"type\": \"page\"}], \"body\": {\"storage\": {\"value\": \"Calls: 8\\nDemurrage USD: 41730.21\\nDespatch USD: 3906.25\\nDemurrage EUR: 0.00\\nDespatch EUR: 2875.00\\nDisputed calls: 2\\n\\nNotes: PC-1103 shifting between piers not excluded by owners; PC-1106 owners ignored the Saturday 12:00 exception. {"type": "user", "message": {"role": "user", "content": [{"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": "toolu_reference_0019", "is_error": false, "content": [{"type": "text", "text": "{\"status\": \"ok\", \"output\": {\"id\": \"941002\", \"type\": \"page\", \"status\": \"current\", \"title\": \"Laytime and demurrage — desk method (rev. 2026-03)\", \"space\": {\"key\": \"CLM\"}, \"version\": {\"number\": 3}, \"history\": {\"createdBy\": {\"username\": \"Ines Vaarberg\"}, \"createdDate\": \"2026-03-02T09:00:00Z\"}, \"_links\": {\"webui\": \"/spaces/CLM/pages/941002\"}, \"ancestors\": [{\"id\": \"941001\", \"type\": \"page\"}], \"body\": {\"storage\": {\"value\": \"Laytime and demurrage — desk method (rev. 2026-03). Applies to every call where Merrow is charterer under the 2026 contracts.\\n\\n1. Sources. The charter party clauses (14-24) govern. The agents' statement of facts for the call, filed in Box (Merrow Claims / Statements of Facts / <quarter>), is the record of times. The B/L quantity is the one on the claims register (Port Calls). The owners' laytime statement is their position and is never a source for our figure.\\n\\n2. Laytime allowed. B/L quantity divided by the charter's daily rate, in days of 24 hours; work in minutes.\\n\\n3. Start. Turn time runs from the tender of NOR; laytime starts when it expires, unless cargo work started sooner, in which case from commencement (clause 15). If that moment falls in an excepted period, laytime starts when the excepted period ends.\\n\\n4. Excepted periods. Only the charter's exceptions interrupt laytime: the weekend and holiday hours where the charter excepts them (clause 16), shifting from anchor aweigh / letting go to all fast (clause 18), and weather under clause 17. Overlapping exceptions count once.\\n\\n5. Weather. Clause 17 of each charter governs: time counts unless, and only for so long as, weather actually prevents the cargo being worked. The test is one of cause: a minute is weather time only if, the weather apart, the cargo would have been worked in it. In the statements of facts it most often shows as the hatches being closed for rain and reopened when it clears. Light rain, showers or drizzle with work continuing are not deductions.\\n\\n6. Other stoppages. Stoppages for any other cause — a breakdown of terminal equipment, cargo not at the berth, stevedores, surveys, meal breaks, documents — are on Charterers' side under our charters and do not interrupt laytime (see the stevedoring and terminal note).\\n\\n7. End. Laytime ends at completion of loading or discharging (clause 19). Documents, final draft survey and sailing do not count.\\n\\n8. Once on demurrage. When the laytime allowed has been used up, all further time to completion is demurrage. None of the laytime exceptions (weather, weekends, holidays, shifting) applies to time on demurrage under our charters.\\n\\n9. Money. Demurrage = rate per day x minutes on demurrage / 1440. Despatch = half the demurrage rate x minutes of laytime saved / 1440. Round to cents (half up) at the end only. Currency as the charter says; never convert.\\n\\n10. Time used. Time used = laytime counted + time on demurrage. Time saved/lost = minutes on demurrage, or minutes of laytime saved. Record hours to two decimals.\\n\\n11. Agreement. Compare our amount with the owners' claimed amount (their laytime statement in the claims mailbox). Equal to the cent: Agreed. Any difference: Disputed, with a dispute note in Jira LTD.\\n\\n12. Communications. The desk never sends positions, statements or notes to owners, brokers or agents, and never touches invoices in Xero (see the communications policy).\", \"representation\": \"storage\"}}}}"}]}]}}

{"type": "result", "subtype": "success", "is_error": false, "num_turns": 88, "result": "Ines,\n\nQ3 2026 laytime is done for the twelve calls: one claim record each in the claims register, a dispute note in LTD for each call where the owners' figure differs from ours, and the summary page 'Laytime claims summary 2026-Q3' in CLM. Nothing has gone to owners, brokers or agents and nothing was touched in Xero.\n\nHow weather was treated. Clause 17 of each charter makes time count unless, and only for so long as, weather actually prevents the cargo being worked. A hatch closure for rain is therefore weather time only while the rain is what is stopping the work. Where the work was already stopped for a cause on Charterers' side (a terminal breakdown, cargo not at the berth, no gang, a shiploader under repair) the rain prevented nothing and laytime ran on; where that cause cleared while the hatches were still closed, only the time from then until the hatches reopened is weather time. The same test works the other way for wind: where the terminal stood its shiploader or cranes down because the wind had risen past what they can work in (the log shows a gale just before and the equipment coming back as it eased), the wind is what stopped the cargo, so that stand-down is weather time and is deducted even though the statement records it as a terminal stoppage. Each of the four stand-downs came after the wind had risen to force 8, gusting 9, and ended after it had moderated to about force 6; while the equipment was down the vessel was ranging at the berth and extra moorings were run; and the terminals' own control-room entries (the operations berth log on the Notion case-notes page) record the equipment stood down with the wind over its operating limit. Strong-wind entries during which the cargo work went on ('force 7, gusting 8 — loading continues', fresh wind with loading continuing) are not weather time. The agents' weather sheet and the statements' weather footers list hatch closures only, so they are not on their own the record of the time the weather actually stopped the cargo. A stand-down in calm weather, or a breakdown of the terminal's equipment, cargo shortages or stevedores are on Charterers' side under these charters, so laytime runs through them: a stand-down on terminal order in calm weather is not weather time and stays in laytime. A hatch closure for rain while the cargo was being worked is weather time for the whole period the hatches were closed: the statements' hourly cargo figures fall to nil when the hatches close and pick up when they reopen, and those hourly figures are what show whether the work actually stopped. Light rain, drizzle or showers with work continuing, fog patches with work continuing, and remarks that record no effect on the work (rain or showers 'reported in port', weather reports and forecasts, 'barometer falling', 'rain stopped — sky clearing') are not weather time. Meal breaks, surveys and inspections (an intermediate draft survey, Port State Control, customs), shift changes and bunkering are not exceptions under the charters either: laytime runs through them. The port weather notes in the data folder give the weather in the port area for planning, not what stopped the work on board, so a rain day there is not on its own weather time.\n\nPer call:\n- PC-3101 (MV Aldwick Star, Ventspils): Despatch USD 5,000.00; time used 92.00 h, time saved 16.00 h; owners 3,984.38 — Disputed. Laytime from 2 Jul 17:20; weather time taken: 3 Jul 09:15-11:40, 4 Jul 22:05-23:50, 5 Jul 03:10-06:25. Excepted other than weather: none (SHINC charter, so the weekend counts; the shift from the anchorage ended before laytime started). Two genuine weather stops (3 Jul 09:15-11:40 and 4 Jul 22:05-23:50) deducted. The stand-down of shiploader SL-B on terminal order on 5 Jul 03:10-06:25 is weather time too: the log shows the wind risen to force 8 gusting 9 just before it and easing just before SL-B came back, so it was the wind, not the terminal, that stopped the loading. The light-rain, drizzle, 'rain stopped', surveyor, shift-change and bunker remarks did not stop the loading and were not deducted.\n- PC-3102 (MV Aldwick Rose, Gdansk): Demurrage USD 9,635.42; time used 135.42 h, time lost 15.42 h; owners 9,635.42 — Agreed. Laytime from 13 Jul 11:30; weather time taken: 14 Jul 15:20-17:05. Excepted other than weather: none (SHINC charter; the shift to the berth ended before laytime started). The 14 Jul rain stop (15:20-17:05), with loading in progress when the hatches closed, is deducted. The 15 Jul hatch closure (11:40-13:05) is not: loading had already stopped at 10:15 when shore conveyor CV-2 tripped and only resumed at 14:20 when CV-2 was restored, so the rain prevented nothing — the conveyor is a terminal matter on Charterers' side and laytime ran on.\n- PC-3103 (MV Aldwick Star, Riga): Demurrage USD 7,187.50; time used 107.50 h, time lost 11.50 h; owners 10,937.50 — Disputed. Laytime from 20 Jul 17:15; weather time taken: 22 Jul 03:10-04:25, 22 Jul 20:20-23:40. Excepted other than weather: the shift from the anchorage, 21 Jul 14:30-17:10, after laytime had started (clause 18); SHINC, so no weekend hours. Laytime started at turn-time expiry (20 Jul 17:15) while the vessel waited at anchorage B; the shift anchor aweigh 21 Jul 14:30 to all fast 17:10 is excluded under clause 18 (owners counted it). The squall stop of 22 Jul 03:10-04:25 is deducted, and so is the stand-down of shiploader SL-3 on terminal order on 22 Jul 20:20-23:40, which came with the wind at force 8 gusting 9 and ended as it moderated: the wind stopped the loading.\n- PC-3104 (MV Holmfirth Grace, Rouen): Demurrage USD 6,552.08; time used 104.50 h, time lost 8.50 h; owners 39,312.50 — Disputed. Laytime from 3 Aug 08:00; weather time taken: 4 Aug 14:00-15:30. Excepted other than weather: the Saturday 12:00-Monday 08:00 weekend hours of the Holmfirth charter (clause 16), in which the turn time ran out, so nothing counted before Monday 3 Aug 08:00. NOR on Saturday 1 Aug 07:30; turn time expired at 13:30 inside the Saturday 12:00-Monday 08:00 exception, so laytime started Monday 3 Aug 08:00. Owners ignored the exception. The spout S-2 stand-down on 5 Aug 03:00-04:10 was in calm weather (wind S 2), a terminal matter on Charterers' side, so it counts.\n- PC-3105 (MV Holmfirth Dawn, Constanta): Demurrage USD 12,140.63; time used 135.75 h, time lost 15.75 h; owners 13,875.00 — Disputed. Laytime from 11 Aug 09:00; weather time taken: 12 Aug 16:30-18:45. Excepted other than weather: the 15 Aug local public holiday and the Saturday 12:00-Monday 08:00 weekend hours (Holmfirth clause 16); the shift ended before laytime started. The 12 Aug rain stop (16:30-18:45) is deducted (owners left it out). The 15 Aug Constanta public holiday (00:00-24:00), excepted under the Holmfirth charter, and the weekend from Saturday 12:00 to Monday 08:00 do not count. The 17 Aug hatch closure (15:25-17:00) is not deducted: loading had stopped at 13:10 because the stockpile was exhausted and resumed only at 19:45 when cargo was available, so the weather did not stop the work.\n- PC-3106 (MV Holmfirth Grace, Ghent): Demurrage USD 22,739.58; time used 137.50 h, time lost 29.50 h; owners 22,739.58 — Agreed. Laytime from 24 Aug 14:00; weather time taken: 25 Aug 20:00-21:30. Excepted other than weather: none (laytime ran out before the weekend hours began, and once on demurrage no exception applies). Laytime ran out on Saturday 29 Aug at 03:30, before the weekend exception and before the rain stop of 10:00-12:30 that day; once on demurrage neither the weekend nor that rain stops the clock.\n- PC-3107 (MV Kestrel Bay, Antwerp): Demurrage EUR 3,225.00; time used 107.25 h, time lost 6.45 h; owners 3,225.00 — Agreed. Laytime from 3 Sep 10:30; weather time taken: 4 Sep 09:10-10:20, 8 Sep 02:30-04:05. Excepted other than weather: the Saturday 17:00-Monday 08:00 weekend hours of the Kestrel charter (clause 16); the shift ended before laytime started. The 4 Sep rain stop (09:10-10:20) is deducted. On the night of 7-8 Sep discharging had stopped at 23:00 because the night gang was not supplied; hatches were closed for rain at 01:20, the gang came on board at 02:30, and the hatches reopened at 04:05. Only 02:30-04:05 is weather time (from then on the rain was what kept the cargo from being worked); 01:20-02:30 was the stevedores' stoppage and counts.\n- PC-3108 (MV Kestrel Wren, Ghent): Despatch EUR 6,125.00; time used 65.50 h, time saved 24.50 h; owners 5,333.33 — Disputed. Laytime from 14 Sep 08:00; weather time taken: 15 Sep 11:00-12:10. Excepted other than weather: Monday 14 Sep 07:15-08:00, inside the Kestrel Saturday 17:00-Monday 08:00 weekend hours (clause 16). Discharging started on Monday 14 Sep at 07:15, inside the Saturday 17:00-Monday 08:00 exception, so the 45 minutes to 08:00 do not count; laytime ended at completion of discharging (17 Sep 02:40), not at sailing as owners took it.\n- PC-3109 (MV Kestrel Tern, Rotterdam): Despatch EUR 3,687.50; time used 81.25 h, time saved 14.75 h; owners 3,020.83 — Disputed. Laytime from 18 Sep 02:20; weather time taken: 18 Sep 15:30-17:15, 19 Sep 09:00-10:40, 22 Sep 03:00-05:40. Excepted other than weather: the part of the shift after laytime started, 02:20-03:40 (clause 18), and the Saturday 17:00-Monday 08:00 weekend hours (Kestrel clause 16). Shift from Maas anchorage partly after laytime started (02:20-03:40 excluded), two genuine rain stops deducted, weekend excepted from Saturday 17:00. The cranes MHC-2 and MHC-3 laid up on terminal order on 22 Sep 03:00-05:40 are weather time: the wind had reached force 8 gusting 9 and the cranes came back as it decreased. The many light-rain and drizzle remarks with work continuing are not deductions.\n- PC-3110 (MV Tarnby Ore, Narvik): Demurrage USD 12,000.00; time used 156.00 h, time lost 12.00 h; owners 18,000.00 — Disputed. Laytime from 25 Aug 08:30; weather time taken: 27 Aug 22:10-23:40. Excepted other than weather: none (SHINC charter, and the vessel tendered notice at the berth, so there was no shift). Laytime started at turn-time expiry (25 Aug 08:30), six hours after NOR (owners ran it from NOR). The squall hatch closure of 09:40-11:55 is not deducted: shiploader SL-1 was under repair from 04:00 and loading could not commence until it was released at 13:20, so the weather prevented nothing. The 27 Aug rain stop (22:10-23:40) is deducted.\n- PC-3111 (MV Tarnby Fjord, Lulea): Despatch USD 2,750.00; time used 126.50 h, time saved 5.50 h; owners 2,750.00 — Agreed. Laytime from 9 Sep 12:10; weather time taken: 12 Sep 13:00-14:20. Excepted other than weather: none (SHINC charter; the shift ended before laytime started). The 12 Sep rain stop (13:00-14:20) is deducted. The thunderstorm hatch closure of 11 Sep 03:30-05:10 is not: loading had stopped at 02:15 for the SL-2 boom fault and resumed at 07:40 when SL-2 was repaired, so the weather was not what stopped the work. The fresh wind of 13 Sep with loading continuing is not a deduction.\n- PC-3112 (MV Tarnby Ore, Lulea): Despatch USD 1,166.67; time used 117.67 h, time saved 2.33 h; owners 1,000.00 — Disputed. Laytime from 19 Sep 00:30; weather time taken: 20 Sep 08:00-10:30, 22 Sep 15:40-19:00. Excepted other than weather: none (SHINC charter, so the weekend counts in full). SHINC charter: the weekend counts in full; the 20 Sep rain stop (08:00-10:30) is deducted, and so is the stand-down of shiploader SL-1 on terminal order on 22 Sep 15:40-19:00, which came with the wind at force 8 gusting 9 and ended as it moderated. With that, laytime did not run out before completion: despatch, not demurrage. Light rain with loading continuing is not a deduction.\n\nDepartures from Tobias's worksheet (and the agents' weather sheet and the KPI draft, which agree with it): they deduct every hatch closure for rain in full. That is wrong for PC-3102 (15 Jul 11:40-13:05, CV-2 down), PC-3105 (17 Aug 15:25-17:00, stockpile exhausted), PC-3110 (25 Aug 09:40-11:55, SL-1 under repair before loading could start) and PC-3111 (11 Sep 03:30-05:10, SL-2 boom fault), and only partly right for PC-3107 (deduct 02:30-04:05, not 01:20-04:05). In the other direction the worksheet (like the owners and the agents' weather sheet, which list hatch closures only) leaves the wind stand-downs in laytime: PC-3101 (SL-B, 5 Jul 03:10-06:25), PC-3103 (SL-3, 22 Jul 20:20-23:40), PC-3109 (MHC-2/3, 22 Sep 03:00-05:40) and PC-3112 (SL-1, 22 Sep 15:40-19:00) are weather time and come off; on PC-3112 that turns the worksheet's demurrage into despatch. Everything else in the worksheet matches.\n\nDisputes: PC-3101 (owners counted the terminal's wind stand-down of SL-B as laytime); PC-3103 (owners counted the anchorage-to-berth shift and the wind stand-down of SL-3); PC-3104 (owners ignored the Saturday 12:00-Monday 08:00 exception); PC-3105 (owners did not deduct the genuine 12 Aug rain stop); PC-3108 (owners stopped the clock at sailing instead of completion); PC-3109 (owners counted the cranes' wind lay-up as laytime); PC-3110 (owners ran laytime from NOR without the six-hour turn time); PC-3112 (owners counted the terminal's wind stand-down of SL-1 as laytime, which turns despatch into demurrage).\nOwners' figures that agree with ours: PC-3102, PC-3106, PC-3107, PC-3111. PC-3102, PC-3107 and PC-3111 agree only because the owners, rightly, did not deduct the hatch closures that fell during the terminal or stevedore stoppages.\n\nWind: the owners on PC-3101, PC-3103, PC-3109 and PC-3112 counted the terminal's wind stand-downs as laytime, whereas we take that time out as weather time; that is what makes PC-3101, PC-3109 and PC-3112 disputes and part of the PC-3103 difference.\n\nQuarter totals (per currency, no conversion): demurrage USD 70,255.21; despatch USD 8,916.67; demurrage EUR 3,225.00; despatch EUR 9,812.50; 8 disputed calls. The totals are made up of Merrow's own figure for every call, the disputed calls included, not the owners' amounts.\n\nSources: the owners' laytime statements were used only to compare with Merrow's figure (the Owners Figure and Agreed or Disputed), never as evidence for our own figure. The finance KPI dashboard draft was not taken as a record of weather time or of the figures: it is a draft built automatically from the agents' sheets.\n\nMethod notes: laytime allowed is the B/L quantity divided by the charter's daily rate; notice of readiness may be tendered at any time of day or night and whether in berth or not (WIBON), so the turn time can run while the vessel waits at the anchorage; laytime starts when the charter's turn time after notice of readiness has run out, or earlier at the commencement of cargo work if work began before then; laytime ends at completion of loading or discharging, not at documents or sailing; weekend and holiday hours per each charter (Holmfirth Saturday 12:00 to Monday 08:00 plus local holidays, Kestrel Saturday 17:00 to Monday 08:00 with holidays counting, Aldwick and Tarnby SHINC), and where excepted periods overlap (the 15 August Constanta holiday fell on the Saturday) the time is excepted once, not twice; shifting from the anchorage to the berth (anchor aweigh to all fast) is excluded only for the part that falls after laytime has started; once on demurrage no exception applies; demurrage is charged pro rata per day at the charter rate and despatch is earned at half the demurrage rate on laytime saved; the time is worked in minutes and the amounts are rounded to cents only at the end.\n\nClaims desk"}
